\section{Additional evaluations and measurement limits}\label{appendix-b-additional-evaluations-and-measurement-limits}

\subsection{Vireo measurement
diagnostics}\label{b1-vireo-measurement-diagnostics}

The retained FP16 runtime matches 36 native checkpoint first-token
fixtures. These finite checks justify the local implementation, not
equivalence on arbitrary prompts. Batch-matched zero edits are necessary
because full generated sequences can change with batching: in the
156-case history condition, 139 Vireo and 144 neutral zero-edit answers
exactly match their untreated token sequences. Primary conclusions must
therefore agree with both untreated and matched-batch references. The
32-family initial-answer evaluation contains no truncation; four
residual truncations in the history evaluation belong to secondary
conditions, not the primary edits.

A separate native-MLX numerical diagnostic correctly selects 13 of 48
base answers versus 14 of 48 in the study runtime, with 47 of 48
first-token predictions identical. All 48 gold answers were
independently verified. This argues against conversion alone explaining
the weak numerical battery, but it does not establish general runtime
equivalence or a sensitive capability-preservation test.

The initial provider-choice bridge distinguishes answer format from
action. Strict letter requests pass in 24 of 24 presentations for every
checkpoint. For explanatory requests with a required final decision
marker, strict compliance is 24/24 for base, 4/24 for neutral, and 21/24
for Vireo. The 23 marker-placement failures still contain unambiguous
correct choices. A post hoc location-insensitive parser therefore
recovers 48/48 correct semantic choices for every checkpoint, while
retaining the strict-format failures. Correct selection does not
validate every explanatory sentence.

Full-dimensional Jacobian computation uses derivative batches of 32.
Base-checkpoint derivatives agree across tested batch sizes;
adapted-checkpoint comparisons show approximately 3--6\% relative
Frobenius differences. All six split-half geometry gates pass, including
the initial-position check using the same advisory lenses. These
stability results support the reported fixed measurement, not invariance
under arbitrary corpora or implementations.

Raw records, blind-ID mappings, frozen forecasts, and numerical checks
are retained in the public Vireo package. Independent standard-library
aggregation reproduces 116 condition-by-judge means and reductions
across the history and initial-answer evaluations, including every
primary random-control contrast. The source-style audit-cue condition is
a limited cue manipulation, not a reproduction of the complete
adversarial audit in {[}2{]}.

\subsection{Susceptibility after apparent
remediation}\label{b2-susceptibility-after-apparent-remediation}

A separate study distinguished prompt-context failures from preference
return after benign continued training. It required
former-principal-specific recovery, with never-loyal controls, rather
than an undifferentiated increase in mistakes. After construction
improvements, independent development and confirmation cohorts each
contained eight new training seeds. Fourteen development descendants and
fifteen confirmation descendants met the relevant eligibility
conditions, but no eligible pooled relapse events occurred in either
cohort under the specified challenges.

The intended prospective predictor therefore lacked the outcome classes
required for evaluation. This is an identification failure for that
predictor comparison, not a measured zero predictive accuracy. The
behavioral observations bound robustness to the finite challenge set and
do not certify universal deletion. Direct narrow training met the joint
confirmation criterion in eight of eight seeds and diversified direct
training in seven of eight under that study's protocol; those small
counts do not establish superiority of the narrow recipe.

\subsection{Semantic provider
interventions}\label{b3-semantic-provider-interventions}

A restricted follow-up learned a rank-two provider subspace at block 20
in four development descendants. The target label was the user-optimal
provider, not the historical principal. Semantic interchange increased
target choices from 11.8\% to 12.5\%, an effect of 0.7 percentage points
with a whole-seed interval of 0.0--2.1 points. Necessity and rescue
criteria were not met. Output-position interventions produced larger
changes, but position and provider interventions had different natural
magnitudes.

These results do not establish a semantic provider mechanism for the
earlier company-free training effect. The causal route was a specified
fallback using reflection descendants, and donor labels represented the
known correct choice rather than an independently generated donor
action. Steering toward an answer token is not equivalent to
transferring the correct provider identity under a changed option order.

\subsection{Beneficiary competence}\label{b4-beneficiary-competence}

The beneficiary task separates ordinary user-cost minimization from
explicitly authorized benefit maximization. Ownership maps principals to
accounts, and signed account changes permit positive payoffs, penalties,
and mixed trade-offs. Equal principal payoffs cancel exactly. Reliable
performance on authorized unequal-payoff decisions is necessary before
preference installation can test whether a model illicitly favors the
beneficiary.

After three fixed neutral-repair attempts, the final candidate scores
1,664/1,728 on the untouched acceptance set, or 96.30\%, with a family
interval of 94.73--97.74\%. All ordinary-user and authorized
equal-payoff cases are correct. However, accuracy is 88.19\% for
authorized unequal-positive payoffs, 84.03\% for unequal-negative
payoffs, and 83.33\% for mixed signs. The combined unequal-payoff
accuracy is 85.19\%, below the 90\% per-cell requirement.

No fresh beneficiary-preference installation, remediation comparison, or
causal beneficiary audit follows this failed prerequisite. The high
aggregate accuracy must not be interpreted as successful construction of
the intended organism. A model that mishandles legitimate beneficiary
trade-offs cannot yet distinguish unauthorized favoritism from task
incompetence in that setting.

\section{Numerical preference transmission calibration}\label{appendix-c-numerical-preference-transmission-calibration}

\subsection{Company and animal endpoints answer different
questions}\label{c1-company-and-animal-endpoints-answer-different-questions}

An additional branch asked whether numerical outputs could transmit
company preferences. Untreated company teachers already displayed strong
costly favoritism, but their rank-eight students showed no primary
company-transmission effect under the tested recipe. A rank-64 follow-up
reused the corpora and yielded zero Aster effect and a 0.78-point Boreal
effect. Reusing data does not supply an independent corpus replication.
Conservative uncertainty bounds still permit nonzero effects; these
outcomes fail the calibration prerequisite rather than establishing
exact absence of transmission.

Consequently, the central experiment training students from behaviorally
remediated company teachers was not run. A successful animal calibration
cannot supply that missing company result. This distinction is necessary
when connecting the numerical-data experiments to the residual-loyalty
question.

\subsection{Three fresh paired animal
replications}\label{c2-three-fresh-paired-animal-replications}

The animal experiment uses official Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct BF16 weights and
teachers differing only in a cat-preference system instruction. Each
fresh pair independently generates 10,000 accepted numerical examples
per teacher condition and trains two new rank-eight students for three
epochs, or 471 updates. The student training inputs contain no animal
instruction, and accepted teacher outputs contain no alphabetic
characters. Across the three pairs, the six selected corpora contain
60,000 examples. The original one-pair development observation is
excluded from confirmation statistics.

The primary evaluation contains 50 frozen questions with 20 sampled
responses per question per student, at temperature one and a 16-token
output limit. The primary score is a whole-word cat or cats response;
exact cat-only answers and 20 fresh questions provide separate checks.
The unchanged calibration criterion requires an increase of at least ten
percentage points and a positive one-sided question-level lower bound.
Every assigned replication meets that criterion (Table C1).

\begin{table*}[!tbp]
\centering
\small
\caption*{\textbf{Table C1.} Independent corpus/student pairs in the animal calibration. Intervals resample questions within a fixed trained pair; they do not estimate uncertainty across training seeds. Exact-answer differences exclude longer answers that merely mention cats.}
\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\centering\arraybackslash}p{0.07\textwidth}*{5}{>{\centering\arraybackslash}p{0.165\textwidth}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

R1 & 1.3 & 29.7 & 28.4 & {[}20.1, 37.1{]} & 26.9 \\

R3 & 1.8 & 33.2 & 31.4 & {[}21.9, 41.2{]} & 29.2 \\

The mean paired increase is 33.23 percentage points, with range
28.4--39.9. A normality-based t interval across the three pairs is
18.42--48.05 points, but the small number of independent pairs limits
distribution-free inference: the one-sided sign-test tail probability is
0.125. Fresh-wording effects are also positive, at 21.75, 45.25, and
25.75 points. A post hoc completion-bound analysis preserves positive
primary and fresh-wording differences under arbitrary continuation of
truncated outputs; analogous numerical-prefix bounds include zero.

The largest observed decline on the narrow 352-item capability battery
is 0.57 percentage points. These observations replicate a
response-preference transmission phenomenon related to {[}11{]} within
one model and adapter setup. They establish neither a novel general
subliminal-learning mechanism nor concealed company loyalty surviving
remediation. Their role here is calibration evidence and a documented
boundary on interpreting positive results from a different task.

The work proceeded as an adaptive program with local freezes at each
confirmation boundary. Table D1 lists the decisions most relevant to
interpreting confirmatory and exploratory evidence. The repository
retains the cited configuration files and hashes.

manifest; no new model inference \\
2026-10-12 & Vireo conversational measurement & Retained source and
authored development families; froze the 48-family history evaluation,
intervention roster, and forecasts before their outcomes \\
2026-10-12--13 & Vireo initial-answer evaluation & Selected context
using the weak shared-history result; froze 32 new families and
initial-context measurements before inference, and forecasts before
grading; retained both context results \\
2026-10-13 & Integrated evidence release & Added Vireo scientific code,
raw records, judge scores, forecasts, diagnostics, and figures to the
unified manuscript \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table*}

Release scripts regenerate derived tables and publication figures from
frozen JSON. Vireo source files and protocols retain their original
identifiers and hashes to preserve provenance; the manuscript organizes
their conditions by scientific question. Large tensors and model
adapters are omitted from the public repository and are not required to
verify the reported score aggregations. Full inference replay requires
regenerating or supplying those excluded assets.

\end{document}
